A program that\n20\t * touches anything else (the kernel's memory, the screen's, an I/O port) is\n21\t * stopped by the processor itself, and the kernel says so on the console.\n22\t * Plan 9.13, findings 0021. It does not run\n9\t   programs in ring 3, it gives every program the kernel's own pages from\n10\t   1 MiB up (share.c, `low_pt`), and only a kernel built with share has any\n11\t   of it: the plain loader has flat segments, no paging and no interrupt\n12\t   table.  Findings 0019 #7 says tests/programs/screencheat.c writes to\n13\t   0xb8000 and gets through; the first megabyte is not mapped under share,\n14\t   so that line, and the words \"first megabyte mapped\" in 0019 #7, were\n15\t   wrong as written (corrected here, 0019 left as signed).  The real hole is\n16\t   that a program runs at ring 0 and can reach the ports and the kernel's\n17\t   pages.  Claude first told Chris the kernel had no paging and no interrupt\n18\t   table; that was true only of the plain loader.\n19\t\n20\t2. **Ruled by Chris, 2026-10-18.**  Paging, not segments: this is a modern\n21\t   operating system and it is done the way it is meant to be done, unless the\n22\t   other is needed for something else.  Segments could not hide the kernel in\n23\t   any case: it sits at 1 MiB, below the programs at 4 MiB, and a segment can\n24\t   only cut off the top.  The system calls without a Linux number yet get\n25\t   AiOs-only numbers for now, as marked tech debt that has to be removed.\n26\t\n27\t3. **The plan.**  A new part, PART.gate@1, owns the descriptor table with ring 3\n28\t   segments, the TSS, the interrupt table, the int 0x80 entry, the fault\n29\t   handlers, and the page tables (kernel pages closed to programs, program\n30\t   pages open).  PART.share@2 depends on it and keeps only the scheduling and\n31\t   the per-program memory; its page-table code moves into the gate, the way\n32\t   sharing came out of the loader in part 16.  PART.loader@9 always has the\n33\t   gate, so a single program is in ring 3 too.  The programs are not\n34\t   recompiled: the table they are handed has the same seven entries in the\n35\t   same places, and each now holds a small stub that runs int 0x80.  Before\n36\t   anything moves, the tests below are written and shown red; the share\n37\t   refactor has to keep TST.share.separate@1 and the ladder green.\n38\t\n39\t4. **The specs and tests, drafted for Chris to decide.**  SPEC.gate.1 to 4;\n40\t   TST.gate.screen-refused, port-refused, kernel-refused, int80, ladder and\n41\t   debt-listed.  Each test's runner names a script that does not exist yet, so\n42\t   each is red now, for the right reason: nothing has been built.  None has a\n43\t   mutation yet.\n44\t\n45\t5. **Tech debt.**  A line of source that is there on purpose and has to go is\n46\t   marked `TECH-DEBT n` in a comment, with what replaces it and when it goes,\n47\t   and is listed here under the same number.  SPEC.gate.4 and\n48\t   TST.gate.debt-listed check that the two match.  Nothing else in the repo\n49\t   uses the mark.\n50\t\n51\t   TECH-DEBT 1.  The system call numbers 1000 (alloc), 1001 (ms), 1002 (load)\n52\t   and 1003 (save) are ours.  Linux's 32-bit x86 numbers stop well short of\n53\t   1000, so nothing clashes.  They go when part 20 brings a loader for Linux's\n54\t   program files: alloc becomes brk, ms becomes clock_gettime, load and save\n55\t   become open, read, write and close.  Until then a Linux program that asks\n56\t   for one of those four gets an error, not a guess.\n57\t\n58\t   TECH-DEBT 2.  A system call runs with interrupts off, so a program waiting\n59\t   inside `read` for a byte stops every other program too, and the stopwatch\n60\t   freezes with it.  Left on purpose.  The fix (let the timer tick in while the\n61\t   read waits) is already written and is the first post of the mechanic, after\n62\t   the series.  No test runs two programs with one of them waiting for input.\n63\t\n64\t   TECH-DEBT 3.  A program's window is rounded up to whole 4 MiB pages, so when\n65\t   the end of its memory is not on a 4 MiB line, the rest of that page is open\n66\t   to it as well: memory it was never given.  Left on purpose.  The mechanic\n67\t   closes it, after the series, by aligning the window or using 4 KiB pages at\n68\t   the edge.\n69\t   TECH-DEBT 4.  (Added in part 20, findings 0023 item 10, decision 0046.)  The\n70\t   16 bytes PART.loader@10 hands a Linux program as AT_RANDOM come from the\n71\t   processor's cycle counter: they are not random, and nothing on AiOs is yet.\n72\t   musl uses them for its stack canary and nothing else here; cat is built\n73\t   without one.  It goes when AiOs has a source of randomness worth the name.\n74\t\n75\t6. **Not decided here.**  Whether the plain loader's single program gets the\n76\t   same page-table layout as a shared one (Claude's pick: yes, one layout, so\n77\t   there is one thing to test), and whether the screen's memory is ever given\n78\t   back to a program (no; the kernel draws, the program asks).\n79\t\n80\t7. **Built, 2026-10-18** (on the workspace's emulator; not yet on the jig).\n81\t   PART.share@2 sits on the gate.  The gate now holds the page directories (one\n82\t   per program, the same virtual window mapped to that program's own RAM), the\n83\t   interrupt table with the 8259's vectors, a 16 KiB kernel stack per program\n84\t   where the processor puts a trap's frame, and the saving and resuming of\n85\t   frames.  Share keeps only the turns: the tick, what to do when a program\n86\t   exits or is stopped, and the cpio reader.  A new flavor, gateshare\n87\t   (petitions 0004, 0006 and 0011), runs the same stopwatch, sieve and probes\n88\t   as the share flavor on PART.loader@9.  PART.loader@8 and PART.share@1 are\n89\t   untouched: petitions add, never swap.  Two differences from share@1 worth\n90\t   knowing: a fault in the kernel itself (ring 0) halts the machine with the\n91\t   Guru line instead of stopping \"the running program\", and the gate allows\n92\t   four programs, as share@1 did.  The plain single program still enters on\n93\t   the 4 KiB boot stack, as it always has; sharing programs get 16 KiB."

Each switch\n27\t * is HAVE_ and the part's name without its version, set by the builder:\n28\t *\n29\t *   HAVE_TIMER_KVMCLOCK   PART.timer.kvmclock@1   the hypervisor says how fast the counter ticks\n30\t *   HAVE_TIMER_TSC_PIT    PART.timer.tsc-pit@N    the counter timed against the 8254\n31\t *   HAVE_FS_MINIX         PART.fs.minix@N         load and save, on PART.disk.ata-pio@1\n32\t *   HAVE_SHARE            PART.share@2          more than one program at once, behind the gate\n33\t *   HAVE_SCREEN_VGA       PART.screen.vga@1       the screen, a second console\n34\t *\n35\t * With both clocks the kernel asks the hypervisor first and times the 8254 if\n36\t * there is no answer, as @7 did.  That is a choice made at run time between\n37\t * two parts, which plan 5.8 says only a petition-requested chooser may make\n38\t * (Linux's clock-source selection, lifted); it is not lifted yet, and the\n39\t * order is still ours (findings 0018 #2).\n40\t *\n41\t * Every console gets every byte written, the way Linux's printk goes to every\n42\t * console it has: the serial port always, and the screen when a petition names\n43\t * it.  The screen follows the few ANSI escapes a program may use for colour\n44\t * (ESC [ ... m), \"home\" (ESC [ H) and \"clear\" (ESC [ 2 J); the serial port is\n45\t * handed the escapes as they are, and a terminal on the other end follows them\n46\t * itself.  Anything else after an ESC is dropped from the screen.\n47\t *\n48\t * A system call whose part is absent answers the way it does when the hardware\n49\t * is missing: ms says 0 forever, load and save say -1.  The table itself never\n50\t * changes shape (door.h).\n51\t *\n52\t * Exposes CAP.proc.exit and CAP.mem.alloc. No `movl $1000+, %eax` in `PART.gate@2/gate_user.S`\n3. When a Linux program calls 1000-1003, gets -38 (ENOSYS)\n4. Old programs still work (via stubs in gate_user.S)\n\nThe approach:\n- In `gate_user.S`: Change stubs to use real Linux numbers\n  - alloc (1000) → brk (45)\n  - ms (1001) → clock_gettime (265) - or another unused number \n  - load (1002) → a suitable Linux number\n  - save (1003) → a suitable Linux number\n\nWait, but if I use brk (45) for alloc stub, the kernel's `case NR_BRK:` currently does `brk(a)` for Linux programs (brk_lo!=0) and for old programs (brk_lo==0) it returns 0 (wrong for alloc). I need to make it call `calls->alloc(a)` when brk_lo==0.\n\nFor ms, what Linux number to use? Looking at Linux i386: clock_gettime is 265. But the gate doesn't handle 265 yet. When brk_lo==0 (old program), it should call `calls->ms()`. When brk_lo!=0 (Linux program), ENOSYS (since clock_gettime is not yet implemented).\n\nFor load (read from file by name) and save (write to file by name) - these are the harder ones. Linux doesn't have a syscall that does exactly what load/save do. But since the old programs that use them (through the stubs) just need to call them, we need unique numbers the gate can intercept.\n\nLooking at the ladder test - life uses alloc. 0 0\n96\t}\n97\t\n98\t# every catalogue number the flavor's petitions name that has no catalogue entry, one to\n99\t# a line with what kind of thing it is; sorted, so the same flavor gives the same list\n100\tcmd_missing() {\n101\t\tf=$(flavor_file \"$1\")\n102\t\tfor id in $(list_field \"$f\" petitions); do petition_codes \"petitions/$id.toml\"; done | LC_ALL=C sort -u | while read -r c; do\n103\t\t\t[ -e \"$(code_file \"$c\")\" ] && continue\n104\t\t\tcase \"${c%%.*}\" in\n105\t\t\t\tPUR) k=purpose ;; KEY) k=key ;; CAP) k=capability ;; PART) k=part ;; MACHINE) k=machine ;;\n106\t\t\t\tTOOL) k=tool ;; AGT) k=agent ;; SPEC) k=clause ;; TST) k=test ;; SNS) k=sensor ;; FND) k=finding ;;\n107\t\t\t\t*) k=thing ;;\n108\t\t\tesac\n109\t\t\tprintf '%s  %s not in the catalogue\\n' \"$c\" \"$k\"\n110\t\tdone\n111\t}\n112\t\n113\t# prog_flags CODE: the extra compile flags a program's catalogue entry lists in\n114\t# cflags (SPEC.builder.3@1), one to a line, -include=H as -include and H; any\n115\t# flag outside the allowed forms stops the build, naming the program and the flag\n116\tprog_flags() {\n117\t\tfor x in $(list_field \"$(code_file \"$1\")\" cflags); do\n118\t\t\tif printf '%s' \"$x\" | grep -Eqx -- '-std=[a-z0-9]+|-D[A-Za-z_][A-Za-z0-9_]*(\\([A-Za-z_]+\\))?(=[A-Za-z0-9_]*)?'; then\n119\t\t\t\tprintf '%s\\n' \"$x\"\n120\t\t\telif printf '%s' \"$x\" | grep -Eqx -- '-include=[A-Za-z0-9_][A-Za-z0-9_/.@-]*\\.h' && ! printf '%s' \"$x\" | grep -q '\\.\\.'; then\n121\t\t\t\tprintf -- '-include\\n%s\\n' \"${x#-include=}\"\n122\t\t\telse\n123\t\t\t\tdie \"SPEC.builder.3: $1 lists the compile flag '$x', which is not -std=, -D or -include= of a header\"\n124\t\t\tfi\n125\t\tdone\n126\t}\n127\t\n128\t# libc_musl DIR: musl from lifted/PART.libc.musl@1, built the way jig/libc builds\n129\t# the pinned one: the two generated headers from their lifted inputs, every\n130\t# lifted source compiled with musl's own flags for i386, the library, the start\n131\t# files, the headers a program sees, and the divide helper musl needs\n132\t# (PART.divide64@1). do\n142\t\t\to=\"$d/obj/$(echo \"$f\" | tr / @).o\"\n143\t\t\tx=\"\"; case \"$f\" in */__libc_start_main.c|*/__init_tls.c|*/__set_thread_area.s|*/memset.s|*/memcpy.s) x=\"-fno-stack-protector\" ;; esac\n144\t\t\t$CC $mf $x -c -o \"$o\" \"$m/$f\" || exit 1\n145\t\tdone || die \"musl from lifted/ did not compile\"\n146\t\t( cd \"$d/obj\" && ls *.o | LC_ALL=C sort | xargs ar rcs ../libc.a )\n147\t\t$CC $mf -DCRT -fno-stack-protector -c -o \"$d/crt1.o\" \"$m/crt/crt1.c\"\n148\t\t$CC $mf -c -o \"$d/crti.o\" \"$m/crt/i386/crti.s\"\n149\t\t$CC $mf -c -o \"$d/crtn.o\" \"$m/crt/i386/crtn.s\"\n150\t\t$CC -m32 -O2 -ffreestanding -fno-pie -c -o \"$d/divide64.o\" pool/PART.divide64@1/divide64.c\n151\t\t( cd \"$m/include\" && find . -type f ! -name '*.in' ) | while read -r h; a program needs a loader\"; done\n191\t\tloader=$(kernel_for \"$f\" $progs)\n192\t\tlf=$(code_file \"$loader\"); ldir=$(field \"$lf\" source)\n193\t\tout=\"out/$1\"; mkdir -p \"$out\"\n194\t\tCC=${CC:-gcc}\n195\t\tCFLAGS=\"-m32 -std=c11 -ffreestanding -fno-pic -fno-pie -fno-stack-protector -fno-builtin -fno-asynchronous-unwind-tables -mno-sse -mno-mmx -mno-red-zone -mgeneral-regs-only -nostdlib -nostdinc -O2 -Wall -Wextra -Werror\"\n196\t\tLDFLAGS=\"-static -no-pie -nostartfiles -Wl,--build-id=none -Wl,-z,noexecstack\"\n197\t\tif [ -n \"$(list_field \"$lf\" builds_with)\" ];
